{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{50.82153pt}{0.32002pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}~,

(139)

makes the search for bounds easier (while being drastically wrong if one is searching for analytical expressions). In fact, all terms related to one Young diagram with
k
blocks are easily obtained by:

•

if the diagram has one row only, consider the

P^{c}_{m}=\frac{1}{2}(m-1)!

(140)

free cyclic permutations
\sigma

[
77
]
of the indices of the expression

\mathfrak{I}_{i_{1}i_{2}}\mathfrak{I}_{i_{2}i_{3}}\dots\mathfrak{I}_{i_{k}i_{1}}~,

(141)

i.e. by filling the Young diagram’s boxes with the label of the integrals composing the II and applying the rule

\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$i_{1}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$i_{2}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$...$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=15.39995pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=15.39995pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$i_{k}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\crcr}}\kern 1035.4pt}}$}~~\rightarrow~~\sigma(\mathfrak{I}_{i_{1}i_{2}}\mathfrak{I}_{i_{2}i_{3}}\dots\mathfrak{I}_{i_{k}i_{1}})~.

(142)

•

if the diagram has more than one row, one must first fill the boxes with all possible

\prod_{c=2}^{k}\frac{1}{\sigma_{c}!}\prod_{r}{k-(m_{1}+\dots+m_{r-1})\choose m_{r}}

(143)

inequivalent fillings (where the notation is as in Eq. (
131
)), and then write all possible permutation for each row as in the above case.

The number of terms extracted with this procedure is

B_{P_{k}}=\prod_{c=2}^{k}\frac{1}{\sigma_{c}!}\prod_{r}{k-(m_{1}+\dots+m_{r-1})\choose m_{r}}P^{c}_{m_{r}}~,

(144)

and hence much smaller than
N_{P_{k}}
. Finally, by bounding all
\mathfrak{I}_{ij}
with the corresponding
\mathcal{Q}\gamma_{ij}
as prescribed by Eq. (
136
), one trades
N_{P_{k}}
II represented by the diagram with
B_{P_{k}}
tight bounds for them (whether these are upper or lower bounds depends on the parity of
k
). Examples of this procedure are provided in App.s
B
,
C
.

A.2
Loose bound

To obtain loose bounds, let us start by considering one
\mathcal{C}_{k,i}(J_{1},\dots,J_{k})
, with
i\in[0,\mathfrak{C}(k)]
, which is the integral of
k
Wightman functions of the kind
\mathcal{W}_{d}
, and has sign
(-1)^{k}
. Therefore, we can write

\mathcal{C}_{k,i}(J_{1},\dots,J_{k})=(-1)^{k}\int_{J_{1},\dots,J_{k}}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\absolutevalue{\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{p}_i(\bold{u},\bold{s}))}~,

(145)

where
\mathfrak{p}_{i}(\mathbb{u},\mathbb{s})
are some linear combination of the components of
\mathbb{u}=(u_{1},\dots,u_{k})
and
\mathbb{s}=(s_{1},\dots,s_{k})
. Thanks to
A2
, all functions appearing in the integral are now definite positive in the integration region, and hence multiplying and dividing in the integral by
\prod_{i=1}^{k}\absolutevalue{\mathcal{W}(s_i)}
and leaving only the one of the two at numerator inside the integral when maximising gives

0\leq\frac{\mathcal{C}_{k,i}(J_{1},\dots,J_{k})}{(-1)^{k}}\leq\mathcal{Q}^{k}\max_{\cup_{i}J_{i}}\left\{\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{k}\absolutevalue{\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{p}_i(\bold{u},\bold{s}))}}{\prod_{i=1}^{k}\absolutevalue{\mathcal{W}(s_i)}}\right\}~.

(146)

A general strategy to solve the above bounds tight ly and sum them to obtain
\Sigma(J_{1},\dots,J_{k})
is given in App.
A
. Here, we proceed by making the bound expressed in (
146
) easier to handle, at the cost of it being looser. Since all
\mathfrak{p}_{i}(\mathbb{u},\mathbb{s})
are some combination of at most
u_{j}
and
u_{j^{\prime}}
and the relative
s
, we have

\max_{\cup_{i}J_{i}}\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{k}\absolutevalue{\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{p}_i(\bold{u},\bold{s}))}\right\}\leq\absolutevalue{\mathcal{W}(T_{\textrm{off}})}^{k}~,

(147)

meaning that

\gamma^{k}\geq\frac{(-1)^{k}\mathcal{C}_{k,i}(J_{1},\dots,J_{k})}{\mathcal{Q}^{k}}\geq 0

(148)

where we used the
\gamma
defined in Eq. (
96
), which assuming
B1
and
B2
satisfies
\gamma\ll 1
. Moreover, since all
\mathcal{C}_{k,i}(J_{1},\dots,J_{k})
within the same
F_{J_{1},\dots,J_{k}}
have the same sign, it follows from Eq. (
148
) that

\mathfrak{C}(k)\gamma^{k}\geq\absolutevalue{F_{J_1,\dots,J_k}}\geq 0~;

(149)

therefore, following Eq. (
95
), and defining

\mathfrak{L}(n;p)=\begin{cases}\sum_{k~\textrm{even}}^{n}{n\choose k}\mathfrak{C}(k)\gamma^{k}~&\textrm{if}~p=+\\
\sum_{k~\textrm{odd}}^{n}{n\choose k}\mathfrak{C}(k)\gamma^{k}~&\textrm{if}~p=-\end{cases}

(150)

we obtain the loose upper and lower bounds

\frac{1+\mathfrak{L}(n;+)}{1-\mathfrak{L}(n-1;-)}\geq\frac{\mathcal{P}_{n}}{q}\geq\frac{1-\mathfrak{L}(n;-)}{1+\mathfrak{L}(n-1;+)}~.

(151)

Note that the above expression is independent of the specific intervals
(N_{1},\dots,N_{n-1},L)
for which the observations giving the outcomes
1
happened. As mentioned in the main text, as for large
k
we have
[
45
]

\mathfrak{C}(k)\sim\frac{(2k-1)!!}{\sqrt{e}}~,

(152)

the above bounds become meaningless for large
n
. In fact, the highest value of
n
for which the bounds are valid is

\mathcal{N}(q,\gamma)=\min\left\{\mathcal{N}_{1},\mathcal{N}_{2},\mathcal{N}_{3}\right\}~.

(153)

where
\mathcal{N}_{1}
,
\mathcal{N}_{2}
and
\mathcal{N}_{3}
are the largest numbers such that

\begin{cases}1-\mathfrak{L}(\mathcal{N}_{1};-)>0\\
1-\mathfrak{L}(\mathcal{N}_{2}-1;-)>0\end{cases}

(154)

and

q\left[\frac{1+\mathfrak{L}(\mathcal{N}_{3};+)}{1-\mathfrak{L}(\mathcal{N}_{3}-1;-)}\right]<1~.

(155)

Then, for
\mathbf{n}
sufficiently smaller than
\mathcal{N}(q,\gamma)
we have

\mathfrak{L}(n;\pm)\ll 1~,~\forall n\leq\mathbf{n}~,

(156)

and hence there exist
\epsilon_{n},\delta_{n}\ll 1
such that

q(1+\epsilon_{n})\geq\mathcal{P}_{n}(L;N_{1},\dots,N_{n-1})\geq q(1-\delta_{n})~,

(157)

independently of the specific
(N_{1},\dots,N_{n-1},L),~\forall n\leq\mathbf{n}
.

Appendix B
Explicit calculation of tight and loose bounds for
\mathcal{P}_{2}
and
\mathcal{P}_{3}

In this appendix, we study the probability of observing the detector in the excited state during the
L
-th interval, given that it was previously found in the excited state in the
N_{1}
-th one (part 1) and in both the
N_{1}
-th and
N_{2}
-th ones (part 2). In other words, we study the quantities

P(L|N_{1})=\frac{\lambda^{6}}{q}\int_{N_{1}L}\mathcal{W}_{4}(X_{2},X^{\prime}_{2},X_{1},X^{\prime}_{1})~,

(158)

and

P(L|N_{1},N_{2})=\frac{\lambda^{6}}{qP(L|N_{1})}\int_{N_{1}N_{2}L}\mathcal{W}_{6}(X_{3},X^{\prime}_{3},X_{2},X^{\prime}_{2},X_{1},X^{\prime}_{1})~,

(159)

where we used the shorthand notation defined in Eq. (
71
) and Eq. (
81
). Throughout this appendix, hypotheses
A0
-
2
and
B1
-
2
are always assumed to hold. Moreover, we recall from Eq. (
72
) that the first transition probability equals
q
regardless of the interval in which it occurs.

B.1
Second transition

By expanding the
4
-points Wightman function via the Wick’s theorem we get

\mathcal{N}(2)=\frac{(2\times 2)!}{2^{2}\times 2!}=3

(160)

terms. In fact, these were already found in Eq. (
83
), which resulted in Eq. (
87
), i.e.

\mathcal{P}_{2}=q\left(1+\frac{\mathcal{C}_{2}(N_{1},L)}{\mathcal{Q}^{2}}\right)~,

(161)

where

\mathcal{C}_{2}(N_{1},L)=\mathcal{C}_{2,1}(N_{1},L)+\mathcal{C}_{2,2}(N_{1},L)~.

(162)

While we already know that in this case tight and loose bounds are equal, because
\gamma_{ij}=\gamma
for all
i,j
, let us proceed with the procedure defined in App.
A
: doing so will clarify the strategy to be applied in more complicated cases. The only restricted partition of
2
is represented by

(163)

describing

N_{2}=(4-2)!!=2

(164)

integrals, namely
\mathcal{C}_{2,1}(N_{1},L)
and
\mathcal{C}_{2,2}(N_{1},L)
. Performing the substitution (
134
), both integrals are mapped into
(\mathfrak{I}_{N_{1},L})^{2}
. This can also be seen by means of the Young tableau
8
8

8

The difference between the names Young diagram and tableau is only a matter of whether the boxes are empty or filled with labels.

N_{1}

L

(165)

for which the only cyclic permutation is
\mathfrak{I}_{N_{1},L}\mathfrak{I}_{L,N_{1}}=(\mathfrak{I}_{N_{1},L})^{2}
. Finally, using Eq. (
136
) we get

0\leq\mathcal{C}_{2}(N_{1},L)\leq 2\mathcal{Q}^{2}\gamma_{N_{1},L}^{2}=2\mathcal{Q}^{2}\gamma^{2}~;

(166)

when read on
P(L|N_{1})
, the above inequality gives

q\leq P(L|N_{1})\leq q(1+2\gamma^{2})~.

(167)

B.2
Third transition

For the sake of simplicity, let us call
L=N_{3}
. By expanding the
6
-points Wightman function via the Wick’s theorem we get

\mathcal{N}(3)=\frac{(2\times 3)!}{2^{3}\times 3!}=15

(168)

term; these can be written in a compact fashion by expressing Eq. (
159
) as

P(N_{3}|N_{1},N_{2})=\mathcal{Q}^{3}+\mathcal{Q}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{j^{\prime}>j}\mathcal{C}_{2}(N_{j},N_{j^{\prime}})+\mathcal{C}_{3}(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3})~,

(169)

where

\mathcal{C}_{3}(J_{1},J_{2},J_{3})=\sum_{i=1}^{\mathfrak{C}(3)}\mathcal{C}_{3,i}(J_{1},J_{2},J_{3})~,

(170)

and where
\mathfrak{C}(3)=8
. Therefore, we can rewrite
P(N_{3}|N_{1},N_{2})
as

P(N_{3}|N_{1},N_{2})=q\left(\frac{1+\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left(\sum_{j^{\prime}>j}F_{N_{j}N_{j}^{\prime}}\right)+F_{N_{1}N_{2}N_{3}}}{1+F_{N_{1}N_{2}}}\right)~,

(171)

where the
F_{J_{1},\dots,J_{n}}
are those defined in Eq. (
92
). From the above section, we know that

0\leq F_{ij}\leq 2\gamma_{ij}^{2}~.

(172)

To bound
F_{N_{1}N_{2}N_{3}}
, we write its Young diagram

\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 690.4pt}}$}~,

(173)

and fill it with the labels of the integrated intervals to get the corresponding Young tableau. From the latter, we extract the only possible cyclic permutation

\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$N_{1}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$N_{2}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$N_{3}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\crcr}}\kern 690.4pt}}$}~\longrightarrow~\mathfrak{I}_{N_{1}N_{2}}\mathfrak{I}_{N_{2}N_{3}}\mathfrak{I}_{N_{3}N_{1}}~.

(174)

It is worth noting that under the substitution (
134
) all eight terms of
\mathcal{C}_{3}(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3})
are denoted by the above expression. Using Eq. (
136
) gives

8\gamma_{N_{1}N_{2}}\gamma_{N_{2}N_{3}}\gamma_{N_{3}N_{1}}\leq F_{N_{1}N_{2}N_{3}}\leq 0~,

(175)

which, when put in Eq. (
169
) together with Eq. (
172
) (and substituting back
L
), gives the tight bound

q\frac{1-8\absolutevalue{\gamma_{N_1N_2}\gamma_{N_2L}\gamma_{N_1L}}}{1+2\gamma_{N_{1}N_{2}}^{2}}\leq P(L|N_{1},N_{2})\leq q(1+2\gamma_{N_{1}N_{2}}^{2}+2\gamma_{N_{2}L}^{2}+2\gamma_{N_{1}L}^{2})~.

(176)

Finally, we can obtain its looser version by simply noting that

\gamma=\gamma_{01}\geq\gamma_{ij}~,~~\forall i,j~.

(177)

Therefore, the loose bound is

q\frac{1-8\absolutevalue{\gamma}^{3}}{1+2\gamma^{2}}\leq P_{3}\leq q(1+6\gamma^{2})~.

(178)

Once again, we stress that the loose bound is independent from the specific intervals during which the excited outcomes are obtained (as expected).

respectively accounting for

Clearly, as we have four options to do so this steps gives four different expressions for each of the six we already had, for a total of
4\times 6=24
. Next, we only have to pick one of the two points in the remaining interval; continuing the example, if we select the point
u_{4}
we get

of above, for a total of

. Finally, there is no other choice left other than inserting the Wightman function evaluated between the two remaining points; in our example these are

and

for the
12
II over two pairs of intervals labelled by corresponding to the diagram (
180
), given that any row of the Young diagram having two blocks gives an integral that can be factorized into two II, the procedure to build the integrals is as follows. First, we fill the first row of the diagram with all inequivalent choices of two intervals’ labels, i.e.

0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{3}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{4}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{1}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\crcr}}\kern 690.4pt}}$}

(191)

and

\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{1}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{2}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{3}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{4}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\crcr}}\kern 690.4pt}}$}~=~\mathcal{C}_{2}(J_{1},J_{2})\mathcal{C}_{2}(J_{3},J_{4})~.

(192)

Finally, we keep only one copy of each identical Young tableau (where two tableau are said to be identical if they become equal by permuting their rows), hence obtaining

\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{1}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{2}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{3}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{4}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\crcr}}\kern 690.4pt}}$}~=~\mathcal{C}_{2}(J_{1},J_{2})\mathcal{C}_{2}(J_{3},J_{4})~,~\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{1}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{3}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{2}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\crcr}}\kern 690.4pt}}$}~=~\mathcal{C}_{2}(J_{1},J_{3})\mathcal{C}_{2}(J_{2},J_{4})~,~\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{1}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{4}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{2}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$J_{3}$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\crcr}}\kern 690.4pt}}$}~=~\mathcal{C}_{2}(J_{1},J_{4})\mathcal{C}_{2}(J_{2},J_{3})~;

(193)

this removal corresponds to the multiplication by
1/2
of Eq. (
131
), accounting for double counting. As expected, this procedure gives

\frac{1}{2}{4\choose 2}\times 2!!\times 2!!=12

(194)

inequivalent products of IIs. Finally, the procedure to explicitly write these II follows the one presented in the above case giving, for example,

\mathcal{C}_{2}(J_{1},J_{2})=\int_{J_{1},J_{2}}\mathcal{W}(u_{1}-u_{2})\mathcal{W}(u_{1}-s_{1}-u_{2}+s_{2})+\int_{J_{1},J_{2}}\mathcal{W}(u_{1}-s_{1}-u_{2})\mathcal{W}(u_{2}-s_{2}-u_{1})~.

(195)

Finally, it is

\begin{split}F_{J_{i},J_{j},J_{k},J_{l}}&=\frac{\mathcal{C}_{4}(J_{i},J_{j},J_{k},J_{l})}{\mathcal{Q}^{4}}=\\
&=\frac{1}{\mathcal{Q}^{4}}\biggl(48~\textrm{IIs~coming~from~}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 690.4pt}}$}~\biggr)~+\frac{1}{\mathcal{Q}^{4}}\biggl(12~\textrm{IIs~coming~from~}\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to15.39995pt{\vss\hbox to15.00002pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-15.39995pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\vrule height=15.39995pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-15.39995pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 15.00002pt\hrule width=15.79988pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 690.4pt}}$}~\biggr)~.\end{split}

(196)

As discussed in App.
A
, the advantage of expressing
F_{J_{i},J_{j},J_{k},J_{l}}
explicitly in terms of Young diagrams is that it directly allows one to find a tight bound for it. Indeed, once the substitution (
132
) is performed all integrals in Eq. (
196
) give place to products of
\mathfrak{I}_{ij}
that are much easier to evaluate. In particular, from the free cyclic permutations of four intervals (i.e.
P^{c}_{4}=3
) we get

\mathfrak{I}_{ij}\mathfrak{I}_{jl}\mathfrak{I}_{lk}\mathfrak{I}_{ki},~\mathfrak{I}_{il}\mathfrak{I}_{lk}\mathfrak{I}_{kj}\mathfrak{I}_{ji},~\mathfrak{I}_{ik}\mathfrak{I}_{kj}\mathfrak{I}_{jl}\mathfrak{I}_{li}~,

(197)

and from the
{4\choose 2}\frac{1}{2!}=3
inequivalent combinations of the products in Eq. (
193
) we get

\mathfrak{I}_{ij}^{2}\mathfrak{I}_{kl}^{2},~\mathfrak{I}_{ik}^{2}\mathfrak{I}_{jl}^{2},~\mathfrak{I}_{il}^{2}\mathfrak{I}_{jk}^{2}~.

(198)

Hence, using Eq. (
136
) it is easy to show that
F_{J_{i},J_{j},J_{k},J_{l}}
is upper bounded by

\begin{split}F_{J_{i},J_{j},J_{k},J_{l}}&\leq\frac{N_{4}}{P^{c}_{4}}\left(\gamma_{ij}\gamma_{jl}\gamma_{lk}\gamma_{ki}+\gamma_{il}\gamma_{lk}\gamma_{kj}\gamma_{ji}+\gamma_{ik}\gamma_{kj}\gamma_{jl}\gamma_{li}\right)+\frac{N_{2+2}}{{4\choose 2}\times\frac{1}{2!}}\left(\gamma_{ij}^{2}\gamma_{kl}^{2}+\gamma_{ik}^{2}\gamma_{jl}^{2}+\gamma_{il}^{2}\gamma_{jk}^{2}\right)=\\
&=\underbrace{16\left(\gamma_{ij}\gamma_{jl}\gamma_{lk}\gamma_{ki}+\gamma_{il}\gamma_{lk}\gamma_{kj}\gamma_{ji}+\gamma_{ik}\gamma_{kj}\gamma_{jl}\gamma_{li}\right)}_{N_{4}=48~\textrm{terms}}+\underbrace{4\left(\gamma_{ij}^{2}\gamma_{kl}^{2}+\gamma_{ik}^{2}\gamma_{jl}^{2}+\gamma_{il}^{2}\gamma_{jk}^{2}\right)}_{N_{2+2}=12~\textrm{terms}}~;\end{split}

(199)

and lower bounded by 0. Since
\gamma_{ij}\leq\gamma

\forall i,j
, this is a tighter bound than the one obtained by the strategy provided in the main body of the article, i.e.

F_{J_{i},J_{j},J_{k},J_{l}}\leq 60\gamma^{4}~.

(200)

Finally, assuming that we calculated
F_{J_{i}J_{j}}
and
F_{J_{i}J_{j}J_{k}}
in a way similar to the above (see App.
B
), and obtained tight bounds for them, we can obtain a most stringent bound for
P(L|J_{1},J_{2},J_{3})
. By calling
L=J_{4}
, from Eq.s (
93
) and (
95
) it is

P(J_{4}|J_{1},J_{2},J_{3})=q\left[\frac{1+\sum_{j=1}^{4}\sum_{j^{\prime}>j}F_{J_{j}J_{j^{\prime}}}+\sum_{j=1}^{4}\sum_{j^{\prime}>j}\sum_{j^{\prime\prime}>j^{\prime}}F_{J_{j}J_{j^{\prime}}J_{j^{\prime\prime}}}+F_{J_{1}J_{2}J_{3}J_{4}}}{1+F_{J_{1}J_{2}}+F_{J_{1}J_{3}}+F_{J_{2}J_{3}}+F_{J_{1}J_{2}J_{3}}}\right]~.

(201)

Hence, recalling that any
F_{i_{1},\dots,i_{2n}}
is positive and any
F_{i_{1},\dots,i_{2n+1}}
is negative, we can lower bound
P(J_{4}|J_{1},J_{2},J_{3})
by

q\left[\frac{1+F_{J_{1}J_{2}J_{3}}+F_{J_{1}J_{2}J_{4}}+F_{J_{1}J_{3}J_{4}}+F_{J_{2}J_{3}J_{4}}}{1+F_{J_{1}J_{2}}+F_{J_{1}J_{3}}+F_{J_{2}J_{3}}}\right]~,

(202)

and upper bound it with

q\left[\frac{1+F_{J_{1}J_{2}}+F_{J_{1}J_{3}}+F_{J_{1}J_{4}}+F_{J_{2}J_{3}}+F_{J_{2}J_{4}}+F_{J_{3}J_{4}}+F_{J_{1}J_{2}J_{3}J_{4}}}{1+F_{J_{1}J_{2}J_{3}}}\right]~.

(203)

Moreover, by using the ratio defined in Eq. (
96
), we get the realization-independent bounds

\begin{cases}0\leq F_{J_{i}J_{j}}\leq 2\gamma^{2}\\
8\gamma^{3}\leq F_{J_{i}J_{j}J_{k}}\leq 0\\
0\leq F_{J_{i}J_{j}J_{k}J_{l}}\leq 60\gamma^{4}\end{cases}

(204)

which, when read on
P(J_{4}|J_{1},J_{2},J_{3})
, give

\frac{q}{1+6\gamma^{2}}\leq P_{4}\leq q\frac{1+12\gamma^{2}+32\gamma^{3}+60\gamma^{4}}{1-8\absolutevalue{\gamma}^{3}}~,

(205)

regardless of the specific
L=J_{4}
and
(J_{1},J_{2},J_{3})
considered.
